			{ tag: "&lt;div id=&quot;atatags-26942-{{unique_id}}&quot;&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;script&gt;__ATA.cmd.push(function() {__ATA.initDynamicSlot({id: \'atatags-26942-{{unique_id}}\',location: 120,formFactor: \'001\',label: {text: \'Advertisements\',},creative: {reportAd: {text: \'Report this ad\',},privacySettings: {text: \'Privacy\',}}});});&lt;/script&gt;", type: 'belowpost' },

			{ tag: "&lt;div id=&quot;atatags-26942-{{unique_id}}&quot;&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;script&gt;__ATA.cmd.push(function() {__ATA.initDynamicSlot({id: \'atatags-26942-{{unique_id}}\',location: 110,formFactor: \'002\',label: {text: \'Advertisements\',},creative: {reportAd: {text: \'Report this ad\',},privacySettings: {text: \'Privacy\',}}});});&lt;/script&gt;", type: 'top' }

<article id="post-247" class="post-247 post type-post status-publish format-standard hentry category-windows tag-keylogger tag-user-mode has-post-thumbnail fallback-thumbnail">
	<header class="entry-header">
		<h1 class="entry-title">Windows Keylogger Part 2: Defense against&nbsp;user-land</h1>					<div class="entry-thumbnail">
				<img width="656" height="300" src="https://eyeofrablog.files.wordpress.com/2017/06/1.jpg?w=656&#038;h=300&#038;crop=1" sizes="min(656px, 100vw)" loading="eager" decoding="async" title="Windows Keylogger Part 2: Defense against&nbsp;user-land" class="attachment-penscratch-2-featured wp-post-image" />			</div>
		
		<div class="entry-meta">
			<span class="posted-on"><a href="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/" rel="bookmark"><time class="entry-date published" datetime="2017-06-27T15:38:23+00:00">June 27, 2017</time><time class="updated" datetime="2017-06-29T00:43:20+00:00">June 29, 2017</time></a></span><span class="byline"><span class="author vcard"><span class="sep"> ~ </span><a class="url fn n" href="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/author/eyeofrateam/">Eye of Ra</a></span></span>					</div><!-- .entry-meta -->
	</header><!-- .entry-header -->

	<div class="entry-content">
		<p>Now, this is the interesting part. Recall from <a href="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/11/windows-keylogger-part-1-attack-on-user-land/">part 1</a>, I had showed you 4 hooking methods using in Windows user-mode and today we will analyze each of them for answering one question: how to detect it? Let’s see!</p>
<h2>Windows test machine:</h2>
<blockquote><p>Windows 7 x86: version 6.1.7601.17514 Service Pack 1 Build 7601)</p>
<p>ntoskrnl.exe: 6.1.7601.17514 (win7sp1_rtm.101119-1850), md5: 2088D9994332583EDB3C561DE31EA5AD</p>
<p>win32k.sys: 6.1.7601.17514 (win7sp1_rtm.101119-1850), md5: 687464342342B933D6B7FAA4A907AF4C</p></blockquote>
<p>*All offset values and structures I used in this part are from test machine.</p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<h2>Windows Hooking: SetWindowsHookEx</h2>
<p>When we register hook using SetWindowsHookEx, the system saves our hook procedure in a hook chain which is a list of pointers. Because we can register many type of message WH_* so there will be a hook chain for each type of message. Therefore our targets are:</p>
<ul>
<li>The location of hook chains in system’s memory (for WH_KEYBOARD, WH_KEYBOARD_LL message type)</li>
<li>How to find the process name of the found hook</li>
</ul>
<p>For the location of hook chain, I have a magic string:</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
nt!_ETHREAD + 0x0 =&gt; nt!_KTHREAD + 0x088 =&gt; nt!_TEB + 0x40 =&gt; win32k!tagTHREADINFO + 0xCC =&gt; win32k!tagDESKTOPINFO + 0x10 =&gt; win32k!tagHOOK
</pre>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<p>Every structure is clear (thanks for windows symbols :d). Offset values are of my test machine and can be vary on each Windows version and build number (ntoskrnl and win32k.sys)</p>
<p>From nt!_ETHREAD, it must be <strong>a GUID thread</strong>. We can get GUI thread from “explorer.exe” or create a thread for your own.</p>
<p>At the end of magic string, we get a final location of all global hook chains in the system. This is a array pointers of tagHOOK with <strong>16 entries</strong>, the index of array is the value of WH_* message type (actually <strong>index = WH_* + 1</strong>). If entry is not NULL then we found a global hook chain.</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
kd&gt; dt win32k!tagHOOK
+0x000 head             : _THRDESKHEAD
+0x014 phkNext          : Ptr32 tagHOOK
+0x018 iHook            : Int4B
+0x01c offPfn           : Uint4B
+0x020 flags            : Uint4B
+0x024 ihmod            : Int4B
+0x028 ptiHooked        : Ptr32 tagTHREADINFO
+0x02c rpdesk           : Ptr32 tagDESKTOP
+0x030 nTimeout         : Pos 0, 7 Bits
+0x030 fLastHookHung    : Pos 7, 1 Bit

dt win32k!_THRDESKHEAD
+0x000 h                : Ptr32 Void
+0x004 cLockObj         : Uint4B
+0x008 pti              : Ptr32 tagTHREADINFO
+0x00c rpdesk           : Ptr32 tagDESKTOP
+0x010 pSelf            : Ptr32 UChar
</pre>
<p>From _THRDESKHEAD in tagHook we get tagTHREADINFO of the process that set the hook. So we can get process id then process name. Here is another magic string</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
processIdOfHooker = PsGetProcessId(IoThreadToProcess((PETHREAD)(*pCurHook-&gt;head.pti)));
</pre>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<p>The scan result:</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="268" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/1/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg" data-orig-size="772,430" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="1" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-268" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=656" alt="1"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg 772w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=150&amp;h=84 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=300&amp;h=167 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/1.jpg?w=768&amp;h=428 768w" sizes="(max-width: 772px) 100vw, 772px" /></p>
<p>Okay, that’s all we need for hunting the global hook of Windows message. Oh, what about local hook? :d</p>
<p>Here is the magic string for local hook:</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
nt!_ETHREAD + 0x0 =&gt; nt!_KTHREAD + 0x088 =&gt; nt!_TEB + 0x40 =&gt; win32k!tagTHREADINFO + 0x198 =&gt;  win32k!tagHOOK
</pre>
<p>Quite similar to global hook but you can see the location of local hook chains is in tagTHREADINFO structure of process and it will be local at that process. The hook chains in tagDESKTOPINFO is global for all process in the same desktop.</p>
<h2></h2>
<h2>Windows Polling</h2>
<p>Well, I actually don’t have any idea for scanning this type of hooking. Why? Because it reads directly keys state from the internal structure and seems there is no way to check who is reading that.</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="275" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/2/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg" data-orig-size="1041,481" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="2" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-275" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=656" alt="2"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg 1041w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=150&amp;h=69 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=300&amp;h=139 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=768&amp;h=355 768w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/2.jpg?w=1024&amp;h=473 1024w" sizes="(max-width: 1041px) 100vw, 1041px" /></p>
<p>How about API hooking for GetAsyncKeyState(), GetKeyboardState()? Yes, we can detect with API hooking but I don’t like it because a global API hooking for all processes in the system is not a good idea. Using API hooking, we can check the frequency and range of checked keys for keylogging detection.</p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<h2>Raw Input</h2>
<p>We start analyzing with <strong>RegisterRawInputDevices()</strong> function in user32.dll. From this API, it will call <strong>NtUserRegisterRawInputDevices()</strong> in win32k.sys</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="285" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/3/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg" data-orig-size="825,808" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="3" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-285" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=656" alt="3"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg 825w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=150&amp;h=147 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=300&amp;h=294 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/3.jpg?w=768&amp;h=752 768w" sizes="(max-width: 825px) 100vw, 825px" /></p>
<p>After some checks and synchronizes, we go to <strong>_RegisterRawInputDevices()</strong></p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="287" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/4/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg" data-orig-size="804,810" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="4" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=298" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-287" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=656" alt="4"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg 804w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=150&amp;h=150 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=298&amp;h=300 298w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/4.jpg?w=768&amp;h=774 768w" sizes="(max-width: 804px) 100vw, 804px" /></p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="289" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/5/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg" data-orig-size="770,474" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="5" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-289" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg?w=656" alt="5"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg 770w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg?w=150&amp;h=92 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/5.jpg?w=300&amp;h=185 300w" sizes="(max-width: 770px) 100vw, 770px" /></p>
<p>That’s quite clear at here. <strong>PsGetCurrentProcessWin32Process()</strong> return win32k!tagPROCESSINFO structure. It check something at offset <strong>0x1A4</strong>, using windbg we have:</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
kd&gt; dt win32k!tagPROCESSINFO
+0x000 Process          : Ptr32 _EPROCESS
…
+0x1a4 pHidTable        : Ptr32 tagPROCESS_HID_TABLE
+0x1a8 dwRegisteredClasses : Uint4B
+0x1ac pvwplWndGCList   : Ptr32 VWPL
</pre>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<p>A pointer to win32k!tagPROCESS_HID_TABLE. Interesting!!!</p>
<p>The lines in range 20-34 validate the registration data (that will be called HID Request)</p>
<p>The lines in range 36-47 allocate HID Table if it not exist. That means if tagPROCESSINFO-&gt;pHidTable is null, no raw input was be registered in this process.</p>
<p>The lines in range 48-71 set HID request into HID table</p>
<p>The remaining of function is update the flags and restart HID device</p>
<p>Let’s see the function <strong>SetProcDeviceRequest()</strong></p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="293" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/6/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg" data-orig-size="886,796" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="6" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-293" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=656" alt="6"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg 886w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=150&amp;h=135 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=300&amp;h=270 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/6.jpg?w=768&amp;h=690 768w" sizes="(max-width: 886px) 100vw, 886px" /></p>
<p>The system allocates a HID Request and insert it to HID Table</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
kd&gt; dt win32k!tagPROCESS_HID_TABLE
+0x000 link : _LIST_ENTRY
+0x008 InclusionList : _LIST_ENTRY
+0x010 UsagePageList : _LIST_ENTRY
+0x018 ExclusionList : _LIST_ENTRY
+0x020 spwndTargetMouse : Ptr32 tagWND
+0x024 spwndTargetKbd : Ptr32 tagWND
</pre>
<p>There are 3 lists of HID Request that were used for raw input: InclusionList, UsagePageList and ExclusionList. To which list will be inserted, it depends on dwFlags value of tagRAWINPUTDEVICE structure when we call RegisterRawInputDevices();</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="297" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/7/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg" data-orig-size="574,261" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="7" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg?w=574" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-297" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg?w=656" alt="7"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg 574w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg?w=150&amp;h=68 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/7.jpg?w=300&amp;h=136 300w" sizes="(max-width: 574px) 100vw, 574px" /></p>
<p>With keylogger, we using <strong>RIDEV_NOLEGACY | RIDEV_INPUTSINK</strong> flags therefore the list will be <strong>InclusionList</strong>.</p>
<p>The last structure we concerned is win32k!tagPROCESS_HID_REQUEST</p>
<pre class="brush: cpp; title: ; notranslate" title="">
kd&gt; dt win32k!tagPROCESS_HID_REQUEST
+0x000 link : _LIST_ENTRY
+0x008 usUsagePage : Uint2B
+0x00a usUsage : Uint2B
+0x00c fSinkable : Pos 0, 1 Bit
+0x00c fExSinkable : Pos 1, 1 Bit
+0x00c fDevNotify : Pos 2, 1 Bit
+0x00c fExclusiveOrphaned : Pos 3, 1 Bit
+0x010 pTLCInfo : Ptr32 tagHID_TLC_INFO
+0x010 pPORequest : Ptr32 tagHID_PAGEONLY_REQUEST
+0x010 ptr : Ptr32 Void
+0x014 spwndTarget : Ptr32 tagWND
</pre>
<p>We can see usUsagePage, usUsage and spwndTarget are the params in tagRAWINPUTDEVICE.</p>
<p><strong>Bingo!!!</strong> For raw input detection we will:</p>
<ol>
<li>Enumerate all process in the system</li>
<li>With each process, we will traverse pID -&gt; PEPROCESS -&gt; tagPROCESSINFO -&gt; tagPROCESS_HID_TABLE -&gt; tagPROCESS_HID_REQUEST</li>
<li>If we found an entry with usUsagePage = 1 (generic desktop controls) and usUsage = 6 (keyboard) then this process is using raw input keylog.</li>
</ol>
<p>The scan result:</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="303" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/8/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg" data-orig-size="802,437" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="8" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-303" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=656" alt="8"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg 802w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=150&amp;h=82 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=300&amp;h=163 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/8.jpg?w=768&amp;h=418 768w" sizes="(max-width: 802px) 100vw, 802px" /></p>
<h2></h2>
<h2>Direct Input</h2>
<p>When checking direct input, I found some interesting signatures in the process registering the hook.</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="306" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/9-2/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg" data-orig-size="1012,588" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="9" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-306" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=656" alt="9"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg 1012w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=150&amp;h=87 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=300&amp;h=174 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/9.jpg?w=768&amp;h=446 768w" sizes="(max-width: 1012px) 100vw, 1012px" /></p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="307" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/10-2/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg" data-orig-size="972,167" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="10" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-307" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=656" alt="10"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg 972w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=150&amp;h=26 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=300&amp;h=52 300w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/10.jpg?w=768&amp;h=132 768w" sizes="(max-width: 972px) 100vw, 972px" /></p>
<p>With MSIAfterburner.exe, we found some handles related to direct input (<strong>Mutant, Section, Key</strong>). From running threads, we also found a thread of <strong>DINPUT8.dll</strong> (a library of Microsoft DirectInput).</p>
<p><strong>Done!</strong> For direct input detection we will:</p>
<ol>
<li>Enumerate all processes in the system</li>
<li>With each process, enumerate all mutant, section, key that match the handle signatures.</li>
<li>If all handle signatures matched, we get start address of all threads in that process. If start address is in the address range of DINPUT8.DLL then we found the direct input keylog.</li>
</ol>
<p>The scan result:</p>
<p><img data-attachment-id="309" data-permalink="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/attachment/11/" data-orig-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg" data-orig-size="685,393" data-comments-opened="1" data-image-meta="{&quot;aperture&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;credit&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;camera&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;created_timestamp&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;copyright&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;focal_length&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;iso&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;shutter_speed&quot;:&quot;0&quot;,&quot;title&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;orientation&quot;:&quot;0&quot;}" data-image-title="11" data-image-description="" data-image-caption="" data-medium-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg?w=300" data-large-file="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg?w=656" tabindex="0" role="button" loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-309" src="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg?w=656" alt="11"   srcset="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg 685w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg?w=150&amp;h=86 150w, https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/11.jpg?w=300&amp;h=172 300w" sizes="(max-width: 685px) 100vw, 685px" /></p>
<h2></h2>
<h2>Conclusion</h2>
<p>We have a summary table for scanning user-mode keylogger:</p>
<table width="0">
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="185"></td>
<td width="222">
<p style="text-align:center;"><strong>Scan method</strong></p>
</td>
<td width="210">
<p style="text-align:center;"><strong>Scan from</strong></p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="185">Windows Hooking (SetWindowsHookEx)</td>
<td width="222">Structure scanning</td>
<td width="210">Kernel-mode</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="185">Windows Polling</td>
<td width="222">API hooking</td>
<td width="210"></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="185">Raw Input</td>
<td width="222">Structure scanning</td>
<td width="210">Kernel-mode</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="185">Direct Input</td>
<td width="222">Signature scanning</td>
<td width="210">User-mode (Admin required)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

					<p>I have two questions: (1) is &#8220;GUID thread&#8221; just a typo of GUI thread? (2) any idea what idea sort of flags go in tagHOOK?</p>

					<div class="comment-metadata">
						<a href="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/#comment-53"><time datetime="2018-04-15T14:03:38+00:00">April 15, 2018 at 2:03 pm</time></a>					</div><!-- .comment-metadata -->

									</footer><!-- .comment-meta -->

				<div class="comment-content">
					<p>Sorry for replying late!<br />
(1) Well &#8220;GUI thread&#8221; means the thread must be use any Windows GUI functionality, if not  win32k!tagTHREADINFO will be not initialized. You can see the ref here: <a href="https://www.geoffchappell.com/studies/windows/km/win32k/structs/threadinfo/index.htm" rel="nofollow ugc">https://www.geoffchappell.com/studies/windows/km/win32k/structs/threadinfo/index.htm</a><br />
(2) It is HF_GLOBALS, HF_LOCAL or HF_DESTROYED and it related to dwThreadId param in SetWindowsHookEx() function</p>
<p id="comment-like-53" data-liked=comment-not-liked class="comment-likes comment-not-liked"><a href="https://eyeofrablog.wordpress.com/2017/06/27/windows-keylogger-part-2-defense-against-user-land/?like_comment=53&#038;_wpnonce=50fc9b82d5" class="comment-like-link needs-login" rel="nofollow" data-blog="130293694"><span>Like</span></a><span id="comment-like-count-53" class="comment-like-feedback">Like</span></p>

		<script type="text/javascript">
			( function () {

				var setupPrivacy = function() {

					// Minimal Mozilla Cookie library
					// https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Document/cookie/Simple_document.cookie_framework
					var cookieLib = window.cookieLib = {getItem:function(e){return e&&decodeURIComponent(document.cookie.replace(new RegExp("(?:(?:^|.*;)\\s*"+encodeURIComponent(e).replace(/[\-\.\+\*]/g,"\\$&")+"\\s*\\=\\s*([^;]*).*$)|^.*$"),"$1"))||null},setItem:function(e,o,n,t,r,i){if(!e||/^(?:expires|max\-age|path|domain|secure)$/i.test(e))return!1;var c="";if(n)switch(n.constructor){case Number:c=n===1/0?"; expires=Fri, 31 Dec 9999 23:59:59 GMT":"; max-age="+n;break;case String:c="; expires="+n;break;case Date:c="; expires="+n.toUTCString()}return"rootDomain"!==r&&".rootDomain"!==r||(r=(".rootDomain"===r?".":"")+document.location.hostname.split(".").slice(-2).join(".")),document.cookie=encodeURIComponent(e)+"="+encodeURIComponent(o)+c+(r?"; domain="+r:"")+(t?"; path="+t:"")+(i?"; secure":""),!0}};

					// Implement IAB USP API.
					window.__uspapi = function( command, version, callback ) {

						// Validate callback.
						if ( typeof callback !== 'function' ) {
							return;
						}

						// Validate the given command.
						if ( command !== 'getUSPData' || version !== 1 ) {
							callback( null, false );
							return;
						}

						// Check for GPC. If set, override any stored cookie.
						if ( navigator.globalPrivacyControl ) {
							callback( { version: 1, uspString: '1YYN' }, true );
							return;
						}

						// Check for cookie.
						var consent = cookieLib.getItem( 'usprivacy' );

						// Invalid cookie.
						if ( null === consent ) {
							callback( null, false );
							return;
						}

						// Everything checks out. Fire the provided callback with the consent data.
						callback( { version: 1, uspString: consent }, true );
					};

					// Initialization.
					document.addEventListener( 'DOMContentLoaded', function() {

						// Internal functions.
						var setDefaultOptInCookie = function() {
							var value = '1YNN';
							var domain = '.wordpress.com' === location.hostname.slice( -14 ) ? '.rootDomain' : location.hostname;
							cookieLib.setItem( 'usprivacy', value, 365 * 24 * 60 * 60, '/', domain );
						};

						var setDefaultOptOutCookie = function() {
							var value = '1YYN';
							var domain = '.wordpress.com' === location.hostname.slice( -14 ) ? '.rootDomain' : location.hostname;
							cookieLib.setItem( 'usprivacy', value, 24 * 60 * 60, '/', domain );
						};

						var setDefaultNotApplicableCookie = function() {
							var value = '1---';
							var domain = '.wordpress.com' === location.hostname.slice( -14 ) ? '.rootDomain' : location.hostname;
							cookieLib.setItem( 'usprivacy', value, 24 * 60 * 60, '/', domain );
						};

						var setCcpaAppliesCookie = function( applies ) {
							var domain = '.wordpress.com' === location.hostname.slice( -14 ) ? '.rootDomain' : location.hostname;
							cookieLib.setItem( 'ccpa_applies', applies, 24 * 60 * 60, '/', domain );
						}

						var maybeCallDoNotSellCallback = function() {
							if ( 'function' === typeof window.doNotSellCallback ) {
								return window.doNotSellCallback();
							}

							return false;
						}

						// Look for usprivacy cookie first.
						var usprivacyCookie = cookieLib.getItem( 'usprivacy' );

						// Found a usprivacy cookie.
						if ( null !== usprivacyCookie ) {

							// If the cookie indicates that CCPA does not apply, then bail.
							if ( '1---' === usprivacyCookie ) {
								return;
							}

							// CCPA applies, so call our callback to add Do Not Sell link to the page.
							maybeCallDoNotSellCallback();

							// We're all done, no more processing needed.
							return;
						}

						// We don't have a usprivacy cookie, so check to see if we have a CCPA applies cookie.
						var ccpaCookie = cookieLib.getItem( 'ccpa_applies' );

						// No CCPA applies cookie found, so we'll need to geolocate if this visitor is from California.
						// This needs to happen client side because we do not have region geo data in our $SERVER headers,
						// only country data -- therefore we can't vary cache on the region.
						if ( null === ccpaCookie ) {

							var request = new XMLHttpRequest();
							request.open( 'GET', 'https://public-api.wordpress.com/geo/', true );

							request.onreadystatechange = function () {
								if ( 4 === this.readyState ) {
									if ( 200 === this.status ) {

										// Got a geo response. Parse out the region data.
										var data = JSON.parse( this.response );
										var region      = data.region ? data.region.toLowerCase() : '';
										var ccpa_applies = ['california', 'colorado', 'connecticut', 'delaware', 'indiana', 'iowa', 'montana', 'new jersey', 'oregon', 'tennessee', 'texas', 'utah', 'virginia'].indexOf( region ) > -1;
										// Set CCPA applies cookie. This keeps us from having to make a geo request too frequently.
										setCcpaAppliesCookie( ccpa_applies );

										// Check if CCPA applies to set the proper usprivacy cookie.
										if ( ccpa_applies ) {
											if ( maybeCallDoNotSellCallback() ) {
												// Do Not Sell link added, so set default opt-in.
												setDefaultOptInCookie();
											} else {
												// Failed showing Do Not Sell link as required, so default to opt-OUT just to be safe.
												setDefaultOptOutCookie();
											}
										} else {
											// CCPA does not apply.
											setDefaultNotApplicableCookie();
										}
									} else {
										// Could not geo, so let's assume for now that CCPA applies to be safe.
										setCcpaAppliesCookie( true );
										if ( maybeCallDoNotSellCallback() ) {
											// Do Not Sell link added, so set default opt-in.
											setDefaultOptInCookie();
										} else {
											// Failed showing Do Not Sell link as required, so default to opt-OUT just to be safe.
											setDefaultOptOutCookie();
										}
									}
								}
							};

							// Send the geo request.
							request.send();
						} else {
							// We found a CCPA applies cookie.
							if ( ccpaCookie === 'true' ) {
								if ( maybeCallDoNotSellCallback() ) {
									// Do Not Sell link added, so set default opt-in.
									setDefaultOptInCookie();
								} else {
									// Failed showing Do Not Sell link as required, so default to opt-OUT just to be safe.
									setDefaultOptOutCookie();
								}
							} else {
								// CCPA does not apply.
								setDefaultNotApplicableCookie();
							}
						}
					} );
				};

				// Kickoff initialization.
				if ( window.defQueue && defQueue.isLOHP && defQueue.isLOHP === 2020 ) {
					defQueue.items.push( setupPrivacy );
				} else {
					setupPrivacy();
				}

			} )();
		</script>

var windowOpen;
			( function () {
				function matches( el, sel ) {
					return !! (
						el.matches && el.matches( sel ) ||
						el.msMatchesSelector && el.msMatchesSelector( sel )
					);
				}

				document.body.addEventListener( 'click', function ( event ) {
					if ( ! event.target ) {
						return;
					}

					var el;
					if ( matches( event.target, 'a.share-twitter' ) ) {
						el = event.target;
					} else if ( event.target.parentNode && matches( event.target.parentNode, 'a.share-twitter' ) ) {
						el = event.target.parentNode;
					}

					if ( el ) {
						event.preventDefault();

						// If there's another sharing window open, close it.
						if ( typeof windowOpen !== 'undefined' ) {
							windowOpen.close();
						}
						windowOpen = window.open( el.getAttribute( 'href' ), 'wpcomtwitter', 'menubar=1,resizable=1,width=600,height=350' );
						return false;
					}
				} );
			} )();

				function matches( el, sel ) {
					return !! (
						el.matches && el.matches( sel ) ||
						el.msMatchesSelector && el.msMatchesSelector( sel )
					);
				}

				document.body.addEventListener( 'click', function ( event ) {
					if ( ! event.target ) {
						return;
					}

					var el;
					if ( matches( event.target, 'a.share-facebook' ) ) {
						el = event.target;
					} else if ( event.target.parentNode && matches( event.target.parentNode, 'a.share-facebook' ) ) {
						el = event.target.parentNode;
					}

					if ( el ) {
						event.preventDefault();

						// If there's another sharing window open, close it.
						if ( typeof windowOpen !== 'undefined' ) {
							windowOpen.close();
						}
						windowOpen = window.open( el.getAttribute( 'href' ), 'wpcomfacebook', 'menubar=1,resizable=1,width=600,height=400' );
						return false;
					}
				} );
			} )();

<script type='disabled' id='wp-enqueue-dynamic-script:wp-i18n:after:1'>
wp.i18n.setLocaleData( { 'text direction\u0004ltr': [ 'ltr' ] } );
</script>

<script>
(function() {
	'use strict';

	const fetches = {};
	const promises = {};
	const urls = {
		'wp-polyfill': 'https://s1.wp.com/wp-includes/js/dist/vendor/wp-polyfill.min.js?m=1727178113i&ver=3.15.0',
		'wp-hooks': 'https://s0.wp.com/wp-content/plugins/gutenberg-core/v19.4.0/build/hooks/index.min.js?m=1728516282i&ver=84e753e2b66eb7028d38',
		'wp-i18n': 'https://s0.wp.com/wp-content/plugins/gutenberg-core/v19.4.0/build/i18n/index.min.js?m=1728516282i&ver=5baa98e4345eccc97e24',
		'verbum': 'https://s2.wp.com/wp-content/mu-plugins/jetpack-mu-wpcom-plugin/moon/vendor/automattic/jetpack-mu-wpcom/src/build/verbum-comments/verbum-comments.js?m=1726650788i&minify=false&ver=f4b641988445c2b095f4'
	};
	const loaders = {
		'verbum': () => {
			fetchExternalScript('wp-polyfill');
			fetchExternalScript('wp-hooks');
			fetchExternalScript('wp-i18n');
			fetchExternalScript('verbum');
			promises['wp-polyfill'] = promises['wp-polyfill'] || loadWPScript('wp-polyfill');
			promises['wp-hooks'] = promises['wp-hooks'] || loadWPScript('wp-hooks');
			promises['wp-i18n'] = promises['wp-i18n'] || promises['wp-hooks'].then( () => loadWPScript('wp-i18n') );
			promises['verbum'] = promises['verbum'] || Promise.all( [ promises['wp-polyfill'], promises['wp-i18n'],  ] ).then( () => loadWPScript('verbum') );
			return promises['verbum'];
		},
		
	};
	const scriptExtras = {
		'wp-i18n': { translations: 0, before: 0, after: 1 },
		
	};

	window.WP_Enqueue_Dynamic_Script = {
		loadScript: (handle) => {
			if (!loaders[handle]) {
				console.error('WP_Enqueue_Dynamic_Script: unregistered script `' + handle + '`.');
			}
			return loaders[handle]();
		}
	};

	function fetchExternalScript(handle) {
		if (!urls[handle]) {
			return Promise.resolve();
		}

		fetches[handle] = fetches[handle] || fetch(urls[handle], { mode: 'no-cors' });
		return fetches[handle];
	}

	function runExtraScript(handle, type, index) {
		const id = 'wp-enqueue-dynamic-script:' + handle + ':' + type + ':' + (index + 1);
		const template = document.getElementById(id);
		if (!template) {
			return Promise.reject();
		}

		const script = document.createElement( 'script' );
		script.innerHTML = template.innerHTML;
		document.body.appendChild( script );
		return Promise.resolve();
	}

	function loadExternalScript(handle) {
		if (!urls[handle]) {
			return Promise.resolve();
		}

		return fetches[handle].then(() => {
			return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
				const script = document.createElement('script');
				script.onload = () => resolve();
				script.onerror = (e) => reject(e);
				script.src = urls[handle];
				document.body.appendChild(script);
			});
		});
	}

	function loadExtra(handle, pos) {
		const count = (scriptExtras[handle] && scriptExtras[handle][pos]) || 0;
		let promise = Promise.resolve();

		for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
			promise = promise.then(() => runExtraScript(handle, pos, i));
		}

		return promise;
	}

	function loadWPScript(handle) {
		// Core loads scripts in this order. See: https://github.com/WordPress/WordPress/blob/a59eb9d39c4fcba834b70c9e8dfd64feeec10ba6/wp-includes/class-wp-scripts.php#L428.
		return loadExtra(handle, 'translations')
			.then(() => loadExtra(handle, 'before'))
			.then(() => loadExternalScript(handle))
			.then(() => loadExtra(handle, 'after'));
	}
} )();
</script>

</body>
</html>
